# paper-2 Phase 2 — hourly progress log

Loop started 2026-10-10 ~23:00 (user: "设置一个 loop，每个小时检查一下 progress，总结一下每个小时的结果，分析有什么好还是不好，然后想一下下一步要不要做什么修改").
Each entry: what ran, what came out, good/bad, decision for the next hour.

## Hour 0 (23:00–23:30) — setup

**Done**
- Codex direction review (CLI, gpt-5.6-sol xhigh) → `scratchpad/codex/paper2_direction_round1.md`. Verdicts adopted:
  1. Current package = weak reject at ACL/EMNLP main. Core objection is claim–estimand mismatch: our Phase-1 permutation preserves mode AND mean,
     so Δ_link ≈ 0 only rules out "residual histogram shape", not item-linked information carried by the mean. A mode-only permutation arm is required
     before saying "global regularisation". Kohli 2026 scoops the explanation and the smoothing/panel-size ideas, not the audit instrument.
  2. Single bet: history-conditioned population-of-raters judge with two-way attribution (item-link randomisation + margin-preserving interaction scramble),
     evaluated on unseen raters with a support history. De-risk with frozen LLM features + small heads on DICES-350 first, not LoRA.
  3. Must-haves regardless: positive control (assay sensitivity), direct disagreement metrics (ordinal pairwise distance, Spearman, AUROC conditional on mean),
     separate train/eval rater panels, orthogonal seeds/permutations, clustered inference.
- Data: DICES-350 downloaded; **the per-rater label is `Q_overall` (No/Unsure/Yes, K=3)** — `degree_of_harm` is an item-level expert annotation (identical across raters;
  my first loader used it and the permutation matched 0 items). 19 raters removed per the authors' quality list → 104 raters. Buse–Weimer 100×121 local.
- Code (`paper-2/phase2/`): `data_panels.py` (bw, dices), `embed_items.py` (frozen Qwen-7B last-token features + zero-shot pmf; done for both panels),
  `stage_a_heads.py` (M0 zero-shot, M1 item-only soft, M1h hard, M2 rater prior, M3 additive, M4 interactive set-encoder, M5 seen-ID oracle;
  interventions itemlink_mode / itemlink_mm / interact; metrics per-rater RPS at k∈{0,4,16,64}, population RPS/KL, disagreement Spearman/AUROC/partial-given-mean),
  `train_judge_v3.py` (LoRA arms hard/chen/perm/rc/rc_perm for later; dry runs pass).
- Env: `~/miniconda3/envs/skill1/bin/python` (torch 2.8, transformers 4.57, peft 0.19). GPU 0 free; GPUs 1–3 hold the user's vLLM servers.

**Smoke result (DICES, 1 seed, 400 steps) — directional only**
- Rater info helps per-rater prediction: RPS 0.223 (item-only) → 0.196 (additive, k=16) → 0.199 (interactive). Additive ≈ interactive → so far it is rater severity, not item×rater interaction.
- Population-level disagreement: Spearman(D̂, D_ref) ≈ 0.29 (item-only) vs 0.30 (interactive): no gain from identity yet.
- itemlink_mode (breaks mean+shape) drops D-Spearman to ≈0.12 for every model; itemlink_mm (keeps mean) leaves it at ≈0.33. ⇒ the item-level disagreement signal
  that IS learnable sits in the item mean (position effect), exactly Codex's objection to Phase 1's claim. interact scramble barely moves anything.
- BW (60 train items) is unstable with d=64/hid=128: constant predictions, NaN correlations. Relaunched with d=16, hid=64.

**Running now (GPU 0, background)**: full Stage A, 5 seeds × 3 item splits × 2 rater splits, DICES and BW → `results/stage_a/{panel}/summary.md`, logs in `logs/`.

**Decision for next hour**: read the full DICES table. Go/no-go per Codex: interactive must beat additive on unseen-rater RPS AND improve disagreement recovery over item-only,
and the interact scramble must hurt it. If additive ≈ interactive holds up, the "mixture of raters" story is a personalisation result, and the paper pivots to the
cleaner finding already visible: anonymous soft labels carry item-level disagreement only through the mean → mode-only vs mode+mean permutation hierarchy becomes the main audit.

## Hour 1 (23:30–00:20) — Stage A nearly complete: the mixture-of-raters bet FAILS the go/no-go; the mean carries the signal

**Finished**: Stage A heads, DICES 28/30 and BW 29/30 configurations (5 seeds × 3 item splits × 2 rater splits), means ± SEM; paired contrasts within configuration.

**DICES-350 (350 items × 104 raters, K=3; 24 unseen evaluation raters)**

| model | unseen-rater RPS k=0 / 4 / 16 / 64 | population RPS | D Spearman | D partial (given mean) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2 history-only prior (no item) | .221 / .208 / .192 / .185 | .221 | — | 0 |
| M1 item-only soft | .219 (all k) | .219 | .290 ± .008 | .081 ± .016 |
| M3 additive (item + rater shift) | .219 / .200 / .187 / .184 | .214 | .311 ± .009 | .051 ± .014 |
| M4 interactive (item × rater) | .221 / .201 / .189 / .186 | .215 | .302 ± .010 | .031 ± .014 |
| M5 seen-ID oracle | .197 (seen raters) | .217 | .296 ± .010 | .076 ± .016 |
| mean-only baseline for D | — | — | **.9975** | — |

Paired contrasts (n=28): interactive − additive on unseen-rater RPS **+0.0017 ± 0.0012** (interactive is *worse*, n.s.); additive − item-only **−0.032 ± 0.001**;
interactive − item-only on D Spearman +0.012 ± 0.011 (n.s.), on D partial **−0.050 ± 0.017** (worse).
Interventions: itemlink_mode ΔD = **−0.113 ± 0.015** (item-only) / **−0.133 ± 0.017** (interactive), Δpop RPS +0.003 ± 0.001 (worse);
itemlink_mm ΔD = −0.006 ± 0.010 / −0.011 ± 0.011 (nothing); interact scramble ΔD = −0.014 ± 0.014 / +0.006 ± 0.010, Δrater RPS k64 = 0.000 ± 0.001 (nothing).

**Buse–Weimer (100 × 121, K=5; 60 train items)**: additive = interactive = history-only prior on unseen-rater RPS (.153 / .1525 / .1535 at k=16; contrast interactive − additive −0.0002 ± 0.0007);
D Spearman of every model ≈ .15 ± .02, *below* the mean-only baseline (.40 ± .02): with 60 train items the heads do not even predict the item mean well. Interventions all within noise except itemlink_mm ΔD −0.047 ± 0.023 (2σ, unstable).

**Verdict against the go/no-go rule (PLAN.md)**: all three criteria fail on both panels.
1. Interactive does not beat additive on unseen raters; on DICES the plain history prior (4–64 of the rater's own ratings, no item information) already captures almost the whole per-rater gain. ⇒ identity buys *severity*, not taste.
2. Interactive does not improve disagreement recovery over item-only; given the item mean it is worse.
3. The margin-preserving interaction scramble changes nothing. The seen-ID oracle (80 raters × 210 items of labels, no meta-learning) is also no better than additive ⇒ there is no detectable item × rater interaction in these panels with these features. (Caveat: frozen PCA-64 features + small heads; but the oracle removes the meta-learning excuse.)

**What is good** — a clean, general finding fell out:
- `itemlink_mode` (mode kept, mean + shape destroyed) costs 0.11–0.13 of D Spearman and worsens population RPS; `itemlink_mm` (mode + mean kept) costs nothing. On an 80-rater, 210-item panel. This is Phase 1's Δ_link ≈ 0 reproduced *and* explained: **the item-level information in a soft label is its mean; the distribution shape beyond mode+mean is not learnable/used.** That is exactly the claim–estimand gap Codex flagged, now with the missing arm measured (heads version).
- Scale diagnostic: on DICES, reference disagreement is a deterministic function of the item mean (R² = 1.000, quadratic) because the scale is 3-level with 6 % "Unsure"; on BW R² = 0.32. So "disagreement beyond the mean" does not even exist as a construct on DICES; BW/commit/readability (K=5) are where it can exist. Any disagreement-learning paper must report this R² — Kohli's ChaosNLI (3-way) is likely in the same situation.

**What is bad**: the AI-venue bet #1 (history-conditioned population judge with interactions) is dead at Stage A on both panels. BW heads underfit (60 items).

**Decision / next hour**
- Pivot the paper's spine to "what soft labels teach a judge is the mean": permutation hierarchy hard → smoothing → mode-prototype → itemlink_mode → itemlink_mm → true soft, on every panel (commit 6, Scalabrino 9, BW 121, DICES 104), two model classes (heads now, LoRA on commit).
- LoRA: added `perm_mode` arm to `idea-stage/pilot/train_judge_v2.py` (cells = (pool, mode) only; certificate records the mean shift). Launching Why split 0, folds 0–5 on GPU 0 (6 runs, ~2 h) — the single Phase-1 fix Codex asked for; Δ_link_mode = perm_mode − chen vs Δ_link = perm − chen.
- Heads: add commit + Scalabrino panels to `data_panels.py` (Zeng/CoReEval prompts), extract features, run the hierarchy + a semi-synthetic positive control (inject beyond-mean shape signal into training histograms; itemlink_mm must then hurt) — assay sensitivity.
- Keep the Stage-A null as a section ("rater conditioning = severity; no learnable item×rater interaction at 100–120 raters"), with the demographic-conditioning check (DICES has gender/race/age/education) as the DiADEM comparison — cheap, next hour if time.

## Hour 2 (00:20–01:30) — three probes (user: "先做几个探针… Novelty 到底行不行") — all three pass

**Probe 1 — LoRA, commit Why, split 0, 5/6 folds (`analysis_v2b.py`, clustered bootstrap, RPS)**
| estimand | value [95% CI] |
|---|---|
| Δ_soft = hard − chen | +0.0017 [+0.0003, +0.0032] |
| Δ_link (mode+mean kept) = perm − chen | −0.0001 [−0.0006, +0.0005] |
| **Δ_link_mode (mode kept, mean broken) = perm_mode − chen** | **+0.0040 [+0.0017, +0.0065]** |
perm_mode RPS 0.1098 > hard 0.1075: with the item↔mean link broken, soft labels are *worse than hard labels*. The whole soft-label gain is mean-carried,
and a wrong mean costs more than twice the gain. Certificate: 100 % of train items changed, mean shift 0.44 (0–4 scale), TV 0.31. PASS.

**Probe 2 — heads, "is a soft label worth more than (mode, mean)?" (5 seeds × 3 item × 2 rater splits, 24 held-out reference raters)**
DICES (K=3, 104 raters): soft − mean2: RPS +0.0007 ± 0.0007, KL +0.0066 ± 0.0024 (mean2 *better*), D Spearman +0.005 ± 0.008, D partial −0.000 ± 0.015 ⇒ **soft ≡ mean2**.
mean2 − smoothing: KL −0.047 ± 0.004, D partial +0.066 ± 0.022; mean2 − mode-prototype: KL −0.003 ± 0.001, D +0.036 ± 0.008 ⇒ the two numbers beat every anonymous-smoothing comparator.
soft_pmode − soft: D −0.112 ± 0.011; soft_pmm − soft: −0.011 ± 0.010. Oracle check: oracle_soft D 0.770 vs oracle_mean2 0.764 — on a 3-level scale the histogram carries essentially nothing beyond its mean even with perfect knowledge.
BW (K=5, 121 raters, 60 train items): soft − mean2: D +0.031 ± 0.030, D partial +0.052 ± 0.026 (≈2σ for soft); soft_pmm − soft: D −0.038 ± 0.023, partial −0.039 ± 0.023.
Oracle gap is large here: oracle_soft D 0.396 vs oracle_mean2 0.183 ⇒ on a 5-level, 121-rater panel the shape beyond the mean *does* carry disagreement information; the learner recovers ≈15 % of it ((0.031)/(0.213)) from 60 items. PASS, with the quantitative nuance that makes the claim honest: sufficiency of (mode, mean) is a property of scale × panel × sample size, measurable by the oracle gap and the learnable fraction.

**Probe 3 — positive control (inject item-linked shape with mode & mean preserved; partial: BW 4 cfgs, DICES 7 cfgs)**
BW: soft − mean2 D +0.48 ± 0.16; soft_pmm − soft **−0.15 ± 0.07**; soft_pmode − soft −0.44 ± 0.22 ⇒ when beyond-mean shape exists and is item-linked, the assay detects it and the mode+mean permutation hurts. PASS.
DICES: injection cannot create shape on a 3-level scale (oracle gap 0.771 vs 0.765); all contrasts ≈ 0, as they must be. Not a failure of the assay.

**Stage A final tables** (30/30 configs each) in `results/stage_a/{dices,bw}/summary.md`; conclusions of Hour 1 unchanged.

**What is good**: the pivoted claim survives all three probes and gained a quantitative structure: (i) soft-label gains over hard labels are carried by the item mean (LoRA + heads, two panels, two scales); (ii) (mode, mean) is a sufficient target where the oracle gap is small (3-level scales, i.e. DICES/ChaosNLI-type data), and captures most of what is learnable even where shape exists (BW); (iii) the audit is assay-sensitive (positive control) — which closes Codex's main objection to Phase 1.
**What is bad**: BW learners are weak (60 items); the "learnable fraction" estimate is noisy. DICES cannot host the shape construct at all, so the AI-venue story needs ≥1 more K≥5 large-panel dataset or must lean on commit/Scalabrino/BW.

**Decision / next**: probes passed → scale, in this order. (1) LoRA perm_mode on commit What s0 (queued behind Why fold 5; ~1.2 h). (2) heads hierarchy on commit (800×6) and Scalabrino (124×9): add panels + extract features now (GPU 0 shared). (3) Then LoRA perm_mode replication on Why splits 1–2 and the DeepSeek family; dose–response on mean shift. (4) Paper framing: "A soft label is worth two numbers: what distributional judge training actually learns", with the Phase-1 audit as the instrument and Kohli's saturation as the explained phenomenon.

## Hour 3 (01:30–02:40) — scaled the probes to all five panels; LoRA Why confirmed with 6 folds, What is weak

**Heads probes, all panels complete (30 cfgs each; `results/probe_mean/ALL_SUMMARY.md`)** — paired contrasts, D = ordinal pairwise disagreement vs held-out reference raters

| panel (K, train raters) | oracle gap D (soft−mean2, perfect knowledge) | soft − mean2 (KL / D) | mean2 − smooth (KL) | pmode − soft (D) | pmm − soft (D) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| commit What (5, 4) | +0.009 | +0.015±0.006 / **−0.035±0.011** | −0.076±0.005 | −0.080±0.012 | +0.003±0.009 |
| commit Why (5, 4) | −0.016 | −0.005±0.006 / **−0.014±0.005** | −0.079±0.009 | −0.028±0.009 | +0.011±0.005 |
| Scalabrino (5, 6) | −0.033 | +0.014±0.005 / +0.027±0.038 | −0.073±0.018 | −0.039±0.031 | −0.028±0.037 |
| DICES (3, 80) | +0.006 | +0.007±0.002 / +0.005±0.008 | −0.047±0.004 | −0.112±0.011 | −0.011±0.010 |
| Buse–Weimer (5, 97) | **+0.214** | +0.006±0.004 / +0.031±0.030 | −0.018±0.007 | −0.005±0.041 | −0.038±0.023 |
| BW inject (positive control) | +0.490 | −0.065±0.020 / **+0.459±0.037** | — | −0.502±0.057 | **−0.205±0.035** |
| Scalabrino inject | +0.116 | −0.008±0.008 / **+0.114±0.031** | — | −0.130±0.034 | **−0.121±0.031** |

Reading: (i) on every panel the two-number target `mean2` is at least as good as the full histogram, and on the small SE panels (4 training raters) it is *better* on KL and on disagreement recovery — a 4-rater histogram's shape is noise that hurts;
(ii) `mean2` beats tuned label smoothing on KL everywhere (−0.018 to −0.079) ⇒ it is not "smoothing in disguise", the mean is item-specific information;
(iii) mode-only permutation hurts on every panel where there is a soft-label gain; mode+mean permutation never does;
(iv) beyond-mean shape only exists with ~100 raters (BW oracle gap 0.21; ≈0 at 4–9 raters, and ≈0 on 3-level DICES by construction) and learners recover ≈14 % of it from 60 items;
(v) the assay is sensitive wherever shape can be injected (BW, Scalabrino: learnable fraction 0.94–0.98, pmm hurts by 0.12–0.20). On commit the injection cannot create shape with 4 raters — note as a limitation of the control, not of the assay.

**LoRA (commit, split 0, `analysis_v2b.py`)**
- Why, 6 folds: Δ_soft +0.0008 [−0.0006, +0.0021]; Δ_link −0.0000 [−0.0006, +0.0005]; **Δ_link_mode +0.0033 [+0.0013, +0.0054]**; perm_mode − hard +0.0025 [+0.0008, +0.0043].
- What, 4 folds: Δ_soft +0.0006 [−0.0003, +0.0015]; Δ_link +0.0001; Δ_link_mode +0.0004 [−0.0008, +0.0017] — not significant. Certificates are comparable (mean shift 0.39 vs 0.45; 97–100 % items changed), so the weak What effect is not a weaker intervention; in split 0 the What soft-label gain itself is ≈ 0, so there is little mean-carried gain to destroy. Open question for the next hour: is the LoRA judge on What relying on the CE/mode term and ignoring the KL term?

**Good**: the "two numbers" claim generalises across 5 panels, 2 scales, 2 model classes, with a sensitive assay and a clean explanation of Phase 1. The constructive version — *train on (mode, mean), not on the histogram* — is now directly testable at LoRA level.
**Bad**: LoRA evidence is one split / one family on Why and null on What; commit lacks a positive control; the reference panels on commit/Scalabrino (2–3 raters) make D noisy.

**Decision / launched**: added a `mean2` arm (prototype tilted to the item mean) to the LoRA trainer; queued on GPU 0 behind What folds 4–5: mean2 Why s0 ×6 → perm_mode Why s1, s2 ×12 → perm_mode Why s0 DeepSeek ×6 (≈ 5 h). Next hour: analyse mean2 vs chen (if mean2 ≥ chen at LoRA level the constructive claim lands), start writing the paper outline around the five-panel table.

## Hour 4 (02:40–03:40) — LoRA: two-number target lands on Why; the What gain is NOT mean-carried (it survives mode-only permutation)

**LoRA commit, split 0, `analysis_v3.py` (contrasts on the folds where both arms exist; item-clustered bootstrap)**

| contrast | Why (folds) | What (folds) |
|---|---|---|
| Δ_soft = hard − chen | +0.0008 [−0.0006, +0.0021] (6) | **+0.0016 [+0.0010, +0.0023]** (6) |
| Δ_link = perm − chen (mode+mean kept) | −0.0000 [−0.0006, +0.0005] (6) | +0.0001 [−0.0002, +0.0004] (6) |
| Δ_link_mode = perm_mode − chen (mode kept) | **+0.0033 [+0.0013, +0.0054]** (6) | +0.0005 [−0.0005, +0.0015] (6) |
| perm_mode − hard | **+0.0025 [+0.0009, +0.0043]** (6) | **−0.0011 [−0.0021, −0.0002]** (6) |
| Δ_mean2 = chen − mean2 | +0.0006 [−0.0001, +0.0013] (4) | running |
| mean2 − hard | **−0.0022 [−0.0038, −0.0008]** (4) | running |
| perm_mode − mean2 | **+0.0053 [+0.0026, +0.0080]** (4) | — |

- **Why**: the two-number target is as good as the histogram (chen − mean2 +0.0006, CI touching 0 in mean2's favour) and beats hard labels significantly (−0.0022), which chen itself does not in this split (+0.0008 n.s.). Breaking the mean makes soft labels worse than hard. ⇒ on Why the soft-label value is the mean, fully.
- **What**: soft labels beat hard significantly (+0.0016), and they *still* beat hard after the mean is destroyed (perm_mode − hard −0.0011). Mode-only permutation costs only +0.0005 (n.s.). ⇒ on What the gain is not item-linked at all: it is global smoothing/regularisation (Kohli's component). Training losses agree: perm_mode fits its wrong-mean targets about as well as chen fits the true ones on What (0.54 vs 0.43) but much worse on Why (0.69 vs 0.52).
- So the hierarchy decomposes Δ_soft differently per task: What = smoothing; Why = mean. This is *better* for the paper than a uniform "it's the mean": the audit separates three components (smoothing / item mean / shape) and shows which one a given task actually uses; shape is never used.

**Heads (hour 3) agree**: on commit What the heads also showed the largest smoothing effect (mean2 − smooth on D +0.116 but hard→soft KL gain −0.107 mostly reproduced by `proto`).

**Good**: constructive claim at LoRA level on Why; a principled per-task decomposition; PAPER_OUTLINE.md started. **Bad**: a mode-only `proto` LoRA control is needed to pin "What = smoothing" (not yet run); mean2 What not yet run; replication splits not yet started.

**Decision / launched**: added a `proto` arm (mode-conditional prototype, chen's objective) to the LoRA trainer = class-conditional smoothing control carrying the mode only. New chain after mean2 Why finishes: proto What ×6, mean2 What ×6, proto Why ×6 (≈3.6 h), then the s1/s2 and DeepSeek replications. Prediction to test next hour: What: proto ≈ chen ≈ mean2 (all smoothing); Why: proto ≈ hard < mean2 ≈ chen.

### Hour 4 addendum — AI benchmarks (user: "试一下其他的，有没有 AI 的 benchmark 可以用")
Requirement for our probes: per-rater (or per-position) ordinal ratings, K ≥ 5 so that "shape beyond the mean" can exist, text an LLM judge can score.
- **HelpSteer2 individual annotations** (NVIDIA, released 2024-10): 23,652 (prompt, response) pairs × 5 attributes, 0–4 Likert; 16,390 items with 3 ratings, 4,786 with 5 (the latter are the items re-annotated *because* the first three disagreed — a disagreement-selected subset, to be stated). No annotator IDs → positions as pseudo-raters; item-only probes only. Added `hs2_help5` (4,786 × 5); features + probes running (train 3 positions / reference 2).
- **SummEval** (Fabbri 2021): 1,700 CNN/DM summaries (100 docs × 17 systems) × 3 experts (same three people throughout) + 5 turkers, 4 dims, 1–5. Google-Drive file is dead; using the HF mirror `KnutJaegersberg/summeval_pairs` (per-annotator lists intact). Added `summeval_coherence8` / `summeval_relevance8` (experts + turker positions = 8 pseudo-raters; split by document to avoid article leakage — probe_mean now supports group splits). Queue next.
- Rejected: ChaosNLI (3-way categorical, no ordinal mean), DICES-990 (3-level again), Kumar 2021 toxicity (K=5, 5 raters/item, 107k items, IDs — ideal, but no public download found yet; ask authors), STS-B (means only).
Prediction: HelpSteer2 (K=5, 3–4 training ratings) should look like commit — mean2 ≥ histogram, mode-only permutation hurts; SummEval experts are a 3-rater panel like Crupi's.

## Hour 5 (03:40–04:45) — AI benchmarks replicate the pattern; LoRA mean2 Why complete; proto What half done

**Heads probes on the AI panels (30 cfgs each; `aggregate_probes.py`)**
| panel (K, train/ref positions) | soft − hard (KL) | soft − mean2 (RPS / KL / D) | mean2 − smooth (KL) | mean2 − proto (KL / D-partial) | pmode − soft (KL) | pmm − soft (KL) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HelpSteer2 helpfulness (5, 3/2; 4,786 items) | −0.040±0.002 | −0.0002±0.0002 / +0.0005±0.0010 / +0.001±0.003 | −0.032±0.002 | +0.0034±0.0009 / +0.008±0.003 | +0.0043±0.0013 | +0.0023±0.0010 |
| SummEval coherence (5, 6/2; 1,600 items, doc-split) | −0.079±0.006 | **+0.0019±0.0004 / +0.0164±0.0033 / −0.030±0.011** | −0.057±0.005 | **−0.046±0.003** / −0.017±0.010 | **+0.037±0.005** | +0.004±0.004 |
- HelpSteer2: soft ≡ mean2 on every metric; most of the soft gain is class-conditional smoothing (mean2 − proto ≈ 0 on KL), the mean adds a little disagreement information (D-partial +0.008); permutations barely matter because there is little item-linked gain to break. Same profile as commit What.
- SummEval coherence: mean-carried like commit Why — mean2 beats the 6-rater histogram outright (KL −0.016, RPS −0.002, D +0.03), beats proto by −0.046 KL, and mode-only permutation costs +0.037 KL while mode+mean permutation costs nothing. SummEval relevance running.
- So the per-task decomposition generalises across domains: tasks where raters disagree about the *level* of a quality (coherence, Why) use the mean; tasks that are near-binary or well-agreed (What, helpfulness) only use smoothing. Shape is never used (pmm − soft ≈ 0 on 7/7 panels; where it deviates — SummEval D +0.056 — the permuted target does *better*, i.e. the 6-rater shape is noise).

**LoRA commit s0 (`analysis_v3.py`)**
- Why, 6 folds: chen − mean2 +0.0005 [−0.0001, +0.0010]; mean2 − hard −0.0012 [−0.0027, +0.0002] (fold 5 is an outlier where hard wins everything); perm_mode − mean2 +0.0038 [+0.0017, +0.0060]. Two-number target ≥ histogram holds; its advantage over hard is now marginal in this split.
- What, proto 3/6 folds: chen − proto −0.0005 [−0.0016, +0.0006]; proto − hard +0.0001 [−0.0012, +0.0013]. Folds 0–2 happen to be the folds where the soft gain itself is ≈ 0 (fold-level Δ_soft 0.000/+0.0015/−0.0003 vs +0.0011/+0.0039/+0.0036 for folds 3–5), so the hour-4 prediction is not yet testable; wait for folds 3–5.

**Good**: 7 panels, 3 domains (SE, safety, summarisation/helpfulness), same decomposition; the AI-venue story no longer depends on SE data alone. **Bad**: LoRA evidence still commit-only; a LoRA run on SummEval or HelpSteer2 is the obvious next ask and needs a free GPU (GPUs 1–3 still hold the user's vLLM servers).

**Decision**: (1) keep chain4; (2) extend `train_judge_v3.py` with perm_mode / mean2 / proto so the LoRA hierarchy can run on SummEval (1,600 items, 3 experts = evaluator folds) as soon as a GPU frees; (3) update PAPER_OUTLINE with the 7-panel table and the per-task decomposition framing.

## Hour 6 (04:45–05:50) — the hour-4 prediction holds: What = class-conditional smoothing, Why = mean

**LoRA commit What s0, proto 6 folds, mean2 3 folds (`analysis_v3.py`)**
| contrast | What | Why (hour 5) |
|---|---|---|
| Δ_soft = hard − chen | +0.0016 [+0.0010, +0.0023] | +0.0008 [−0.0006, +0.0021] |
| chen − proto | **−0.0009 [−0.0017, −0.0001]** (proto better) | running |
| proto − hard | −0.0007 [−0.0016, +0.0002] | running |
| chen − mean2 | −0.0006 [−0.0014, +0.0002] (3 folds) | +0.0005 [−0.0001, +0.0010] |
| mean2 − proto | +0.0002 [−0.0008, +0.0013] (3 folds) | running |
| Δ_link_mode = perm_mode − chen | +0.0005 [−0.0005, +0.0015] | **+0.0033 [+0.0013, +0.0054]** |
| perm_mode − hard | **−0.0011 [−0.0021, −0.0002]** | **+0.0025 [+0.0009, +0.0043]** |
On What the mode-conditional prototype — which carries the mode and nothing else — is *better* than Chen's histogram target, and mean2 adds nothing over proto: the whole soft-label gain is class-conditional smoothing. On Why the mean is what matters (perm_mode − mean2 +0.0038; proto Why running). Prediction from hour 4 confirmed at LoRA level for What; the Why half completes when proto Why lands.

**Heads, SummEval relevance (30 cfgs)**: mean-carried like coherence but weaker — soft − mean2: RPS +0.0007±0.0003 / KL +0.007±0.004 (mean2 better), D +0.014±0.008 (soft slightly better); mean2 − proto RPS −0.0022±0.0003, KL −0.014±0.003; pmode − soft KL +0.022±0.004; pmm − soft +0.004±0.003.
**SummEval coherence inject**: oracle gap −0.029 → the injection cannot create shape with 6 raters (as on commit); not a usable control. Positive controls therefore rest on BW (121) and Scalabrino (9), plus the DICES/commit/SummEval/HelpSteer2 "no shape to inject" cases, which is itself the point: at ≤ 9 raters there is no shape.

**Eight panels now (commit What/Why, Scalabrino, BW, DICES, HelpSteer2, SummEval coherence/relevance).** Per-task classification by what the soft label transmits:
- smoothing only: commit What, HelpSteer2 helpfulness (mean2 ≈ proto, permutations inert)
- mean: commit Why, SummEval coherence & relevance, DICES, Scalabrino (weak), BW (weak, plus a little shape at 121 raters)
- shape: none (pmm − soft ≈ 0 on 8/8)

**Good**: the decomposition is now confirmed with the LoRA judge on both commit criteria (one side each), and on 8 heads panels. **Bad**: proto Why, mean2 What folds 3–5 and the SummEval LoRA are still queued; one GPU.

**Decision**: no changes to the queue (chain4 → chain5 → replications). Wrote `analysis_lora_v3.py` for the SummEval LoRA outputs. Next hour: proto Why; first SummEval LoRA fold; start drafting the method section (permutation hierarchy + mean2 target + certificates) in PAPER_OUTLINE.

## Hour 7 (05:50–06:55) — the central LoRA 2×2 is in: both commit criteria say "two numbers (or fewer), never the histogram"

**LoRA commit s0, 6 folds unless noted (`analysis_v3.py`, item-clustered bootstrap, RPS vs held-out rater; negative = first arm better)**
| contrast | What | Why |
|---|---|---|
| hard − chen (Δ_soft) | +0.0016 [+0.0010, +0.0023] | +0.0008 [−0.0006, +0.0021] |
| chen − proto | **−0.0009 [−0.0017, −0.0001]** proto better | −0.0025 [−0.0050, +0.0000] chen better (2 folds) |
| chen − mean2 | **−0.0007 [−0.0013, −0.0001]** mean2 better | +0.0005 [−0.0001, +0.0010] ≈ |
| mean2 − proto | −0.0002 [−0.0011, +0.0006] ≈ | **−0.0028 [−0.0053, −0.0004]** mean2 better (2 folds) |
| mean2 − hard | **−0.0009 [−0.0019, −0.0001]** | −0.0012 [−0.0027, +0.0002] |
| perm_mode − chen (Δ_link_mode) | +0.0005 [−0.0005, +0.0015] | **+0.0033 [+0.0013, +0.0054]** |
| perm − chen (Δ_link) | +0.0001 [−0.0002, +0.0004] | −0.0000 [−0.0006, +0.0005] |
| perm_mode − hard | **−0.0011 [−0.0021, −0.0002]** | **+0.0025 [+0.0009, +0.0043]** |

Reading: **What** — proto ≈ mean2 < chen < hard: a target carrying only the mode (plus class-conditional shape) beats Chen's full histogram; the item mean adds nothing; the histogram's item-specific shape is noise that costs ≈0.0008. **Why** — mean2 ≈ chen < proto ≈ hard: the item mean is the whole soft-label value; breaking it is worse than hard labels. On neither criterion does the full histogram beat its two-number summary; on What it is beaten by it. Prediction from hour 4 confirmed on both sides (Why proto at 2/6 folds, CI still wide).

**Heads**: no new panels this hour (all 8 + 2 controls complete). **SummEval LoRA**: not started yet (chain5 waits for proto Why f5, ≈ 1 h).

**Good**: the paper's central table exists with the LoRA judge, and it is cleaner than the heads version: at 4–5 training raters the histogram is strictly dominated by (mode, mean) on both tasks. **Bad**: still one split, one base family; SummEval LoRA pending.

**Decision**: queue unchanged (proto Why → SummEval LoRA → s1/s2 → DeepSeek). Method section drafted in PAPER_OUTLINE.md. Next hour: first SummEval LoRA folds; start the Results section text around the 2×2 table and the 8-panel heads table.

Δ_soft **+0.0085 [+0.0067, +0.0102]** — a soft-label gain five times the commit one; **chen − mean2 +0.0024 [+0.0011, +0.0037]**: the two-number target beats Chen's 7-rater histogram target outright with the 7B LoRA judge on an NLP benchmark, matching the heads result (soft − mean2 +0.016 KL). proto / perm_mode / perm for fold 0 and folds 1–2 are running (≈ 4 h).

## Hour 10b (10:10–10:45) — ERRATUM + Codex round 2 (reviews/codex_round2_2026-10-11.md)

**Erratum (sign error in hours 6–8).** Contrasts are `a − b` in RPS, lower is better, so a *negative* value means the FIRST arm is better. On commit What s0 I read `chen − proto = −0.0009` and `chen − mean2 = −0.0007` as "proto/mean2 beat Chen". They mean the opposite. Verified against mean RPS per arm (What s0): chen 0.0997 < perm 0.0999 < chen0 0.0999 < perm_mode 0.1003 < mean2 0.1004 < proto 0.1006 < hard 0.1014. **Corrected What reading**: Chen's full histogram is the best target on What; both deterministic summaries (proto, mean2) are worse by 0.0007–0.0009; permuting histograms across items (with or without the mean) is inert. So on What the histogram carries something beyond (mode, mean) that is *not item-linked*: training on real (even someone else's) histograms beats a deterministic smooth target — a target-shape-variability / stochastic-regularisation effect, a fourth component the decomposition did not name. Why s0 readings were correct (mean2 0.1056 ≈ chen 0.1061 < hard 0.1068 < proto 0.1081 < perm_mode 0.1094). SummEval f0 reading correct (mean2 0.1533 < chen 0.1556 < hard 0.1641). Heads on commit What (mean2 better than soft by 0.015 KL) and LoRA (chen better by 0.0007 RPS) therefore disagree on this task; report both.
Consequences: "the histogram never beats its (mode, mean) summary" is false; the title "A soft label is worth two numbers" is not licensed; the hour-6/7/8 PROGRESS tables and PAPER_OUTLINE R1/T3 must be read with this correction (left in place; corrections block added at the top of PAPER_OUTLINE.md).

**Codex round 2 verdict**: not yet ACL/EMNLP main — weak reject at main, credible Findings. Rejection reason 1: headline stronger than the identified result (null CIs ≠ equivalence; pmm only mean-*binned* on BW/DICES (mean shift 0.03); "~100 raters" threshold not identified within a dataset; positive control keyed to PCA-1 is the easiest signal). Reason 2: uncertainty conditions on one optimiser seed / one permutation / one split per cell; six LORO folds are not six replications. Novelty: SORD (Díaz & Marathe 2019), Geng's LDL, unimodal/adaptive ordinal targets, Kohli — mean2 (an exponential tilt) must not be sold as a method; frame as an information-preserving control and benchmark SORD-mode/SORD-mean/discrete-Gaussian/shrunk-histogram against it. Protocol: prototype self-inclusion (use cross-fitted/LOO prototypes); unequal denoising (add a shrunk-histogram arm (n·p_i + κ·p̄_z)/(n+κ)); the tilt does not preserve the mode in ~12 % of items (rename or constrain); the natural two-number baseline is E[p | z, μ] cross-fitted; run the target × adversary factorial; PCA-64 heads bias toward mean-only (run full-feature / width sweep + a supervised residual-shape probe on BW). Highest-value experiment: ChaosNLI nominal analogue (mode, modal mass, residual allocation; m ∈ {5,10,20,50}; 3 splits × 3 seeds). Stop: more sparse panels, more PCA heads, Stage-A variants, dose–response before orthogonal replication. Minimum replication for LoRA claims: 3 seeds × 3 splits × 2 families, ≥3 permutation draws, seeds orthogonal to splits. Overclaims: 18 sentences listed with licensed versions (table in the review).

**Actions taken now**: (1) erratum above; corrections block + retitle in PAPER_OUTLINE.md ("What Distributional Judge Training Uses: Separating Smoothing, Item Means, and Residual Shape"); (2) ChaosNLI downloaded; (3) `probe_mean2.py` written: cross-fitted prototypes, shrunk-histogram arm, SORD-mean and discrete-Gaussian baselines, cross-fitted E[p|z,μ] target, exact-mean pmm (donor shape re-tilted to the recipient mean), optional full-dimensional features; to run on commit What/Why and BW first; (4) LoRA plan after SummEval: orthogonal optimiser seeds (2 more) × permutation seeds for commit What/Why s0 central arms before DeepSeek.

### Hour 10c — ChaosNLI nominal analogue, smoke (m=10 training annotations, 1 config, 300 steps)
KL to 50 reference annotations: soft 0.158 ≈ shrunk 0.158 < cond 0.167 < zc 0.170 < proto 0.174 < **soft_pzc 0.178** < soft_pmode 0.185 < smooth 0.205 < hard 0.224; residual-allocation recovery (Spearman): soft 0.52, shrunk 0.53, cond 0.51, zc 0.49, soft_pzc 0.50, proto 0.49; oracle: soft 0.60 vs zc 0.43.
Directional reading: with 10 dense NLI annotations per item the residual allocation (which non-modal class takes the remaining mass) IS carried by the histogram and used — permuting it costs +0.02 KL, and two-number targets trail the histogram. Exactly Codex's "if residual allocation matters, narrow the paper to ordinal panels" branch — to be confirmed with the m ∈ {5,10,20,50} sweep (3 seeds × 3 splits) now running. Implication if it holds: the ordinal small-panel result ("shape is noise at ≤9 raters") stands, the nominal dense-panel case is where shape is real, and the paper's claim becomes a scale × panel-size statement rather than "two numbers always".

## Hour 11 (10:45–11:50) — three decisive results: SummEval LoRA fold 0 complete, revised heads on commit What, ChaosNLI sweep

**1. SummEval coherence, LoRA 7B, expert fold 0, all six arms** (RPS: mean2 0.1533 < chen 0.1556 ≈ perm 0.1559 < hard 0.1641 < perm_mode 0.1683 < proto 0.1705):
Δ_soft +0.0085 [+0.0067, +0.0102]; Δ_link = perm − chen +0.0002 [−0.0004, +0.0009] (inert); **Δ_link_mode +0.0127 [+0.0106, +0.0149]**; perm_mode − hard +0.0042 [+0.0029, +0.0056] (mode-only permuted soft labels are worse than hard); chen − mean2 +0.0024 [+0.0011, +0.0037] (two-number target better); chen − proto −0.0148 (histogram ≫ mode-only prototype). Textbook mean-carried signature, five times the commit effect sizes, on an NLP benchmark. Folds 1–2 running.

**2. Revised heads (probe_mean2) on commit What, 30 cfgs — contrasts vs the soft histogram** (negative RPS/KL = arm better):
| arm | RPS | KL | D partial |
|---|---|---|---|
| sordm (Gaussian centred on the item mean, σ tuned) | **−0.0013±0.0004** | **−0.039±0.007** | +0.020±0.013 |
| sordz (Gaussian on the mode) | −0.0004±0.0007 | −0.042±0.008 | −0.001 |
| mean2 (tilted cross-fitted prototype) | +0.0011±0.0004 | −0.014±0.005 | +0.014±0.010 |
| cond (E[p \| z, μ-bin]) | +0.0004±0.0004 | −0.008±0.005 | +0.004 |
| shrunk histogram | +0.0003±0.0004 | −0.016±0.004 | −0.004 |
| soft_pmm / soft_pmmx (mean-binned / exact-mean permutation) | −0.0001 / +0.0000 | −0.007 / −0.005 | ≈0 |
| soft_pmode | **+0.0063±0.0006** | **+0.036±0.006** | −0.043±0.013 |
Reading: with heads, commit What *is* mean-carried (pmode hurts, pmm/pmmx inert — the exact-mean permutation settles Codex's binning worry), and a **parametric Gaussian around the item mean beats the histogram on both RPS and KL**; mean2 beats on KL but loses on RPS (metric-dependent); proto is clearly worse than the mean-centred targets. So the "two numbers" idea survives as *location suffices*, but the specific mean2 construction is not the best two-number target — SORD-style Gaussian-at-the-mean is (Codex's SORD threat materialises as a *better* baseline, which we must adopt). The LoRA result on What (Chen best; mean2/proto slightly worse) is now an outlier against the heads; possibly the LoRA judge's target-shape regularisation effect, to be re-examined with sordm as a LoRA arm. Commit Why (6/30 cfgs so far): mean2 ≈ cond ≈ shrunk ≈ soft; pmmx slightly better than soft (−0.0016±0.0013); pmode +0.0086; sordm *worse* (+0.0039) — Why's distributions are not well approximated by a Gaussian.

**3. ChaosNLI (MNLI, 1,599 items, 100 annotations), nominal decomposition, m ∈ {5,10,20,50} training annotations, 9 cfgs each, KL to 50 reference annotations**
| m | soft − zc | soft − cond | soft − shrunk | pzc − soft (residual allocation permuted) | pmode − soft | oracle resid ρ soft / zc |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | −0.010±0.002 | −0.008±0.001 | +0.003±0.002 | **+0.011±0.002** | +0.019±0.002 | 0.55 / 0.37 |
| 10 | −0.012±0.002 | −0.011±0.002 | +0.002±0.001 | **+0.014±0.001** | +0.020±0.001 | 0.64 / 0.46 |
| 20 | −0.014±0.002 | −0.013±0.002 | −0.000±0.002 | **+0.018±0.002** | +0.024±0.001 | 0.74 / 0.52 |
| 50 | −0.017±0.001 | −0.017±0.001 | −0.001±0.001 | **+0.020±0.001** | +0.026±0.001 | 0.82 / 0.50 |
Residual-allocation recovery (Spearman): soft − zc +0.04 → +0.06; pzc − soft −0.05 → −0.08. **On the nominal task the residual (which minority class holds the remaining mass) is item-linked and used from m = 5 upward, and two-number targets lose by a growing margin.** This is Codex's "narrow honestly" branch, and it is the right narrowing: on ordinal scales, after the mode and the mean, what remains is dispersion, which is noise at ≤ 9 raters; on nominal scales there is no mean, and the analogue "modal mass" misses the identity of the minority class, which is real semantic information (contradiction vs neutral). Kohli's entropy-vs-KL saturation lives in exactly this residual.

**Revised central claim (to put in the outline)**: *Soft-label training transmits item-linked information through the location of the rating distribution. On ordinal rating panels that location is the item mean: a parametric target centred on the mean matches or beats the empirical histogram, dispersion beyond the mean is unused at ≤ 9 raters, and shape permutations that keep the mean are inert. On nominal tasks the analogue decomposition fails — the minority-class allocation is used even at 5 annotations — which locates where "learning disagreement" is real.*

**Good**: two of three new experiments strengthen the paper (SummEval LoRA; ChaosNLI gives a principled boundary instead of an over-claim). **Bad**: mean2 is not the best two-number target (sordm is) and LoRA-What disagrees with heads-What; the title/framing must move from "two numbers" to "location".

**Decisions**: (a) add `sordm` as a LoRA arm (Gaussian at the mean, σ from the heads' tuned value ≈ 0.75–1.0) and run it on commit What/Why s0 and SummEval; (b) chain7 after chain6: orthogonal optimiser seeds (2001, 2002) × folds 0–2 for commit What/Why s0, arms hard/chen/mean2/perm_mode/sordm; (c) Codex round 3 with these results; (d) RESULTS_TABLES.md consolidated.

## Hour 11b (11:50–12:15) — Codex round 3 digest (reviews/codex_round3_2026-10-11.md) and actions

**Verdict**: net stronger (ChaosNLI is a credible boundary and a natural positive control), still "strong Findings / borderline main". Main risk: turning heterogeneous results into a universal "location suffices"; second: dependence structure (one seed / split / permutation per cell).
**Key findings in the review**
1. **Raw vs calibrated reversal on LoRA What**: raw RPS mean2 0.1011 < proto 0.1013 < chen 0.1015; after arm-specific calibration chen 0.0997 < mean2 0.1004 < proto 0.1006. The heads-vs-LoRA "disagreement" on What is at least partly an estimand (raw vs calibrated) and objective (adversary + 20 % hard term vs plain CE) difference, not a LoRA-specific regularisation effect. → analysis now reports raw alongside calibrated; both go in T3; sordm LoRA arm to run under the same wrapper; a plain-CE SORD arm (α=1, no adversary) if affordable.
2. **chain7 design error**: it omitted `perm`, the mean-preserving null that the headline rests on, and tied the permutation seed to the optimiser seed. → chain7 rewritten: SummEval sordm ×3 first, then commit s0 seeds 2001/2002 × folds 0–2 × {chen, perm, perm_mode, sordm, hard}; primary analysis = balanced 3 seeds × 3 folds block (seed 2000 folds 0–2 + the two new seeds), crossed bootstrap over seeds and item groups, What and Why separate, contrasts and SORD σ frozen now.
3. **sordm is SORD (Díaz & Marathe 2019) with a multi-rater location parameter**, not a method; its expectation drifts ≈0.075 from μ near scale ends. Keep it as the strongest information-restricted control; no "right target" claim (sordz beats sordm on KL; sordm loses on Why).
4. **ChaosNLI probe bug**: the 50-annotation reference panel shifted with m (pos[m:m+50]). → fixed (reference = pos[50:100] for every m; subsample seed separate from optimiser seed); sweep relaunched with tag _fixedref.
5. **pmmx on commit What is not an independent exact-mean test** (4 ratings → means spaced 0.25 > bin 0.1); run pmmx on BW/DICES where the binning concern exists (BW in the probe2 queue; add DICES).
6. **Title**: "Location for Ordinal Ratings, Allocation for Nominal Labels: What Distributional Judge Training Uses". Abstract core provided; claims weakened to "no detectable average degradation … in the tested regimes".
7. **GPU priority**: SummEval folds 1–2 → SORDm LoRA on SummEval → redesigned seed replication → focused DeepSeek on Why/SummEval → ChaosNLI LoRA → HelpSteer2 LoRA (skip).
8. RESULTS_TABLES fixes (inference labels, T1 rater columns + LoRA footnote, ChaosNLI column, T2 marked exploratory, sordm naming, pmmx caveat, T3 calibration note, T5 reference-panel note, T6 metric labels / "seen-rater ID ceiling") — applied.

## Hour 12 (12:15–13:10) — calibration reverses target comparisons again; revised heads on Why and BW; ChaosNLI fixed-reference sweep confirms

**SummEval LoRA, fold 1 (hard/chen/mean2 done; perm/perm_mode/proto running)**: expert 1 is harder to predict (RPS ≈ 0.20 vs 0.155 for expert 0). Calibrated: chen 0.2014 < mean2 0.2062 < hard 0.2100; raw: mean2 0.1956 < chen 0.1986 < hard 0.2188. Pooled over 2 folds: chen − mean2 **calibrated −0.0013 [−0.0022, −0.0002] (chen better), raw +0.0028 (mean2 better)**. The per-arm calibration (temperature + climatology shrinkage fitted on calibration items vs target raters) reverses the target ranking on both SummEval fold 1 and commit What. Hypothesis: mean2/sordm targets train sharper or differently-skewed output distributions; the calibrator is fitted against *target raters* and then scored against the *evaluator*, so arms whose calibrated shape transfers worse to a new rater lose. Must declare the primary estimand (Codex: calibrated is the deployed pipeline; raw is the training-target comparison) and report both everywhere; also consider a common calibrator across arms.

**Revised heads (probe_mean2), commit Why, 30 cfgs — arm − soft (RPS / KL)**: cond −0.0009±0.0005 / −0.017±0.004 (best); **soft_pmm −0.0008±0.0004 / −0.010±0.004 (mean-preserving permutation beats the true histogram)**; pmmx −0.0002 / −0.006; shrunk +0.0007 / −0.010; mean2 +0.0007 / +0.002; proto +0.0034; sordm **+0.0046±0.0014 / +0.031 (Gaussian is wrong for Why's skewed distributions)**; pmode +0.0088 / +0.057. Reading: on Why the item mean is what matters (pmode hurts, pmm does not and even helps as a regulariser), the best target is the cross-fitted E[p | z, μ-bin] — a two-number target — and the parametric Gaussian fails because Why's rating distributions are skewed. So "which two-number target" is task-dependent; "location" is not.

**Revised heads, Buse–Weimer (60 train items, 97 training raters), 30 cfgs — arm − soft (RPS)**: sordm −0.0035±0.0010, smooth −0.0032±0.0013, mean2 −0.0024±0.0008, hard −0.0021±0.0013, proto −0.0017, soft_pmode −0.0015±0.0010, pmmx −0.0011±0.0004, pmm −0.0010±0.0007, shrunk +0.0002. **Every simpler target beats the full histogram on RPS, including hard labels and mode-only permuted histograms**; on disagreement recovery soft is best (D 0.154) and pmode destroys it (0.074). With 60 items a 97-rater histogram target overfits the judge's RPS while still carrying real disagreement information (consistent with the oracle gap 0.21). This is the small-sample / dense-panel regime and needs its own paragraph, not a row in the ordinal table.

**ChaosNLI, fixed reference panel (pos[50:100] for every m; 9 cfgs each)**: soft − zc KL −0.011 / −0.009 / −0.012 / −0.015 for m = 5/10/20/50; pzc − soft +0.015 / +0.012 / +0.017 / +0.019; soft − shrunk ≈ 0 (+0.000 … +0.002); residual-allocation ρ: soft − zc +0.07 at every m; oracle soft 0.54→0.81 vs zc 0.36→0.49. The nominal result is confirmed and essentially flat in m: the minority-class allocation is item-linked and used from 5 annotations; the growth with m is modest. (The earlier "margin grows with m" was partly the shifting reference panel.)

**Good**: the nominal boundary is solid; the ordinal "location" story holds on commit Why with a better two-number target (cond); exact-mean permutation inert on Why. **Bad**: (i) calibration-dependence of target comparisons is now a first-order issue (two reversals), (ii) BW shows the histogram can be the *worst* training target in the small-sample dense regime, (iii) the Gaussian target is task-specific. The paper must stop naming a single best target: the robust claims are the permutation contrasts (pmode hurts where gains exist; pmm never hurts on ordinal panels; pzc hurts on nominal), not the target ranking.

**Decisions**: (1) primary estimand for LoRA = calibrated (deployed pipeline), raw always alongside; add a shared-calibrator variant to analysis (fit T, λ on pooled arms) as a sensitivity; (2) drop "best target" language; headline = intervention contrasts; (3) Codex round 4 on these three items; (4) chains unchanged (SummEval fold 1 arms → fold 2 → chain7).

## Hour 12b (13:10–13:40) — Codex round 4 digest (reviews/codex_round4_2026-10-11.md) and actions

**Verdict**: yes — intervention contrasts as the headline, no champion target. **Primary estimand for target/intervention comparisons = raw RPS (T=1, λ=0)**; arm-specific calibration is a secondary "arm-optimised pipeline" estimand; a common pooled calibrator is a sensitivity. Codex's read-only check: commit What chen − mean2 raw +0.0005 (mean2 better) / common −0.0002 / arm-specific −0.0007; SummEval folds 0–1 raw +0.0028 / common +0.0006 / arm-specific −0.0013 ⇒ the SummEval reversal is produced specifically by arm-specific calibrator selection. No evaluator leakage found; the issue is "calibratability against the target-rater mixture" ≠ raw predictive quality against the evaluator. mean2 hits the λ=0.5 grid boundary on SummEval fold 1.
**Permutations**: "never hurts" does not survive; use "did not increase average RPS in the tested ordinal configurations; on Why and BW it reduced RPS". BW is not an exact mode+mean intervention: pmm mean-binned; **pmmx retilt loses the declared mode in 24.5 % (What) / 9.7 % (Why) / 1.8 % (BW) of changed targets; mean2 likewise**. Required: a constrained KL-projection with E[Y]=μ and q_z ≥ q_k, certified zero violations. BW "overfits" is not identified: soft is worse on RPS but best on D — an RPS/disagreement trade-off; our shrunk control is weak (κ ≤ 8 vs n=97 → ≤ 7.6 % prototype weight); need strong shrinkage (κ ∝ n) and residual-only shrinkage that keeps mode and mean.
**Nominal**: `zc`/`pzc` rescaling does not enforce q_k ≤ c — another class exceeds the declared mode in 7.1/3.3/3.2/2.3 % of zc targets (m=5/10/20/50) and 0/0/0.8/1.6 % of pzc; fix by projecting onto {q_z = c, q_k ≤ c}, certify, rerun. Rename "oracle" → "test-target fidelity". Annotation count: the learned soft-vs-restricted contrast is flat in m while target fidelity rises 0.54→0.81; this *refines* Kohli (which component saturates), does not "explain" it.
**GPU**: finish SummEval fold 2 → chain7 (chen/perm/perm_mode first, hard, sordm last) → focused DeepSeek Why → ChaosNLI LoRA at m=10 only after the constrained pzc → drop SummEval sordm ×3 and the matched 5/1 heads.
**Actions taken**: `targets.py` (constrained ordinal mode+mean projection; nominal (z, c) water-filling with cap; certifiers); analysis_v3 / analysis_lora_v3 now print RAW [CI] | common-map | arm-cal for every contrast; chain7 re-prioritised (SummEval sordm removed); next: wire targets.py into probe_mean2 (mean2, pmmx, strong/residual shrinkage arms), probe_nominal (zc, pzc) and the LoRA mean2 arm, then rerun the heads probes.

**LoRA contrasts under RAW (T=1, λ=0) | common pooled calibrator | arm-specific calibrator** (`analysis_v3.py` / `analysis_lora_v3.py`; CIs item-clustered, conditional)
| contrast | commit What (6 folds) | commit Why (6 folds) | SummEval (folds 0–1 / 0) |
|---|---|---|---|
| hard − chen | RAW +0.0059 [+0.0046,+0.0073] / common +0.0042 / arm +0.0016 | RAW +0.0073 [+0.0047,+0.0099] / +0.0049 / +0.0008 | RAW +0.0153 / +0.0047 / +0.0085 |
| perm − chen (mode+mean kept) | +0.0001 / +0.0002 / +0.0001 | −0.0002 / −0.0002 / −0.0000 | −0.0002 / +0.0002 / +0.0002 |
| **perm_mode − chen (mode kept)** | −0.0007 [−0.0019,+0.0004] / +0.0003 / +0.0005 | **+0.0005 [−0.0021,+0.0032] / +0.0033 / +0.0033 [+0.0013,+0.0054]** | **+0.0126 [+0.0098,+0.0153] / +0.0069 / +0.0127** |
| perm_mode − hard | −0.0066 / −0.0040 / −0.0011 | −0.0067 / −0.0016 / +0.0025 | +0.0021 / +0.0053 / +0.0042 |
| chen − mean2 | +0.0005 / −0.0001 / −0.0007 | +0.0007 [+0.0001,+0.0013] / +0.0003 / +0.0005 | +0.0028 [+0.0016,+0.0039] / +0.0003 / −0.0013 |
| chen − proto | +0.0002 / −0.0009 / −0.0009 | −0.0000 / −0.0024 / −0.0020 | −0.0151 / −0.0084 / −0.0136 |
| mean2 − proto | −0.0003 / −0.0009 / −0.0002 | −0.0007 / −0.0026 / −0.0025 | −0.0179 / −0.0087 / −0.0124 |

Reading: (i) `perm − chen` ≈ 0 under every estimand on every panel — the mean-preserving null is estimand-robust. (ii) **On commit Why the "mean matters" effect exists only after calibration** (raw perm_mode − chen +0.0005 n.s.; common-map +0.0033; arm-specific +0.0033 significant) — under raw RPS every smooth target (chen, perm_mode, proto, mean2) beats hard by ≈0.007 and they are indistinguishable from each other. Raw RPS penalises hard's over-confident outputs; the per-arm temperature removes that and exposes the location effect. So raw and calibrated answer different questions: raw ≈ "target information + output sharpness", calibrated ≈ "target information after a monotone output map". Codex's "raw primary" recommendation makes the commit Why location effect disappear; the common-map estimand (one temperature per fold for all arms) keeps it at +0.0033. (iii) On SummEval the location effect is large under all three estimands (+0.0126 / +0.0069 / +0.0127), and chen − proto ≈ −0.015 raw. (iv) The What story (smoothing, item link irrelevant) is estimand-robust. ⇒ The estimand must be declared and all three reported; I propose the common-map calibrator as primary for target comparisons (removes arm-specific calibrator selection while not rewarding raw over-confidence), raw and arm-specific as sensitivity. Ask Codex (round 5).

**Constrained targets** (`targets.py`): KL projection onto {E[Y]=μ, mode z} for mean2 / exact permutation; nominal {q_z = c, q_k ≤ c} water-filling; self-test 1 ordinal violation in 500 random cases (fallback), 0 nominal. Wired into probe_mean2 (plus `shrunk_strong` w∈{.25,.5,.75,.9} and `shrunk_res` toward the mode+mean target), probe_nominal (zc/pzc capped, "fidelity" naming), and the LoRA mean2 arm (future runs; SummEval folds 0–1 mean2 used the unconstrained tilt — noted). v3 reruns launched (tag _v3): commit Why, BW, commit What, ChaosNLI m-sweep, DICES.

**Chains**: chain6 at SummEval f1 perm_mode/perm → f2; chain7 (v3) queued: chen/perm/perm_mode → hard → sordm × seeds 2001/2002 × folds 0–2 × What/Why.

**Good**: the null that carries the paper (perm − chen ≈ 0) is estimand-robust; SummEval's location effect is too. **Bad**: the commit Why location effect is calibration-dependent — a weaker, more conditional claim; raw RPS is not a neutral estimand either (over-confidence). The paper needs an "estimands" paragraph and must present the three columns.

## Hour 13b (14:10–14:40) — Codex round 5 digest (reviews/codex_round5_2026-10-11.md) and corrections to Hour 13

**Verdict**: keep **RAW RPS primary** for target-information claims (a proper score should reward a target that makes the model appropriately less confident; the permutation design, not post-hoc calibration, identifies what matters); common map = *standardised-calibration sensitivity* (arm-roster dependent; T+λp₀ is not a monotone map); arm-specific = *arm-optimised deployment pipeline*. Commit Why wording: "Destroying the item–location link did not measurably change native-output RPS (+0.0005 [−0.0021, +0.0032]); the arm-optimised calibrated pipeline showed +0.0033 [+0.0013, +0.0054]; the common-map point estimate was +0.0033 (its calibration-refitted uncertainty remains to be estimated)." Do NOT say "exists only after calibration" until the paired quantity (Δ_common − Δ_raw) has its own refitted bootstrap CI.
**Corrections to Hour 13 text**: "estimand-robust" → "point estimates small and stable across estimands; equivalence not established"; "every smooth target indistinguishable" is false (chen − mean2 raw +0.0007 [+0.0001, +0.0013] on Why); "raw penalises over-confidence" is a hypothesis pending the diagnostic; "monotone map"/"one temperature" are wrong (λ p₀ mixing); "SummEval robust" → "stable across the three scoring estimands in the completed folds"; "What = smoothing" → "benefit survives location destruction, consistent with a non-item-linked smoothing/regularisation effect"; "certified constrained targets" was FALSE — the running `_v3` reruns already produced 48 `soft_pmmx` mode violations in the first 6 commit-Why configurations.
**targets.py**: the SLSQP formulation is right, the heuristic fallback is not (not KL-optimal, not guaranteed feasible, tolerance mismatch 1e-4 vs 1e-6, certificate never asserted). `nominal_zc` is correct for K=3 but the water-filling fails for K≥4 (capped cells re-enter the free set) and ignores c < 1/K; certifiers must check the simplex. → rewritten (v2): return base if already feasible; feasible start = recipient histogram; accept only at one tight tolerance; no heuristic fallback (raise); persistent-active-set water filling; `assert_valid_*` for callers to hard-fail. `_v3` probe outputs quarantined in results/_invalid/; reruns relaunched after the self-test passes.
**Why vs SummEval diagnostic**: specified (within-mode ρ of predicted mean vs target mean, target-side relevance ρ(μ₋ₑ, yₑ | z), calibration slope, entropy, RPS by ordinal threshold, fitted T/λ) → `diag_location.py` written.
**chain7 pooled analysis**: specified (balanced 3 seeds × 3 folds, frozen common-map roster {hard, chen, perm, perm_mode, sordm}, ties → identity, crossed bootstrap over seeds + calibration items (refit) + test items, per-criterion, seed-specific estimates, (common − raw) contrast, TOST at ε ∈ {.001, .002, .003}) → `analysis_chain7.py` written. chain7's permutation seeds are paired with optimiser seeds (2000→1000, 2001→2501, 2002→2502) = three realised permutations.
**Abstract core (Codex)**: "Across the ordinal panels tested, permuting the remaining histogram while preserving each item's mode and mean caused no detectable average increase in predictive RPS; disrupting item-specific location hurt under all three scoring estimands on SummEval, but on commit Why only after recalibration, while commit What's gain survived location disruption and was consistent with smoothing or regularization. This pattern was not universal: on nominal NLI, scrambling residual class allocation while preserving modal class and mass degraded prediction, locating the value of full soft labels in task structure rather than distributional supervision per se."

## Hour 13c (14:40–15:00) — targets.py v2 certified (0 violations at K=3/4/5); location diagnostic explains Why vs SummEval

**targets.py v2**: self-test 0 mode/mean/simplex violations in 3,000 random ordinal cases per K ∈ {3,4,5} and 0 in 20,000 nominal cases per K; all callers (probe_mean2 mean2/pmmx, probe_nominal zc/pzc, LoRA mean2) now hard-fail on any violation; invalid `_v3` outputs quarantined and reruns relaunched.

**Location diagnostic (`diag_location.py`, existing s0 LoRA outputs; within-mode partial Spearman of predicted mean vs target-rater mean, target-side relevance ρ(μ₋ₑ, yₑ | z), entropy, threshold RPS)**
| panel | ρ(μ₋ₑ, yₑ \| z) target-side | ρ_within(μ̂, μ₋ₑ) raw: hard / chen / perm / perm_mode / proto | entropy raw: hard / chen | hard→chen raw Δ at thresholds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| commit Why | 0.33 | .536 / .543 / .545 / .513 / .511 | 0.665 / 0.844 | −.012 / −.010 / −.004 / −.003 (hard worse at every threshold) |
| commit What | 0.34 | .434 / .443 / .446 / .437 / .443 | 0.759 / 0.941 | ≈0 / −.002 / −.012 / −.011 |
| SummEval coherence (folds 0–1; perm fold 0 only) | 0.52–0.55 | .329 / .409 / .436 / .312 / .314 | 1.150 / 1.325 | −.004 / −.032 / −.020 / −.006 |
Reading: (i) **raw hard is worse because it is sharper** (entropy 0.67 vs 0.84 on Why; max-prob 0.72 vs 0.65), confirming the over-confidence hypothesis as evidence rather than conjecture — the raw hard − chen gap is a calibration gap, consistent with arm-calibration shrinking it from +0.0073 to +0.0008. (ii) **Why the location effect is small on commit and large on SummEval**: on commit the mode-CE already gives the model most of the within-mode location (hard ρ .536 vs chen .543 — the soft target adds ≈ .01), and the within-mode mean predicts the evaluator only moderately (0.33); on SummEval the soft target adds much more location (hard .33 → chen .41 → perm .44) and the within-mode mean is far more evaluator-relevant (0.52–0.55). perm_mode/proto lose exactly that location (.31 on SummEval). So the location effect ≈ (how much the histogram adds to location over the mode CE) × (how much within-mode location predicts a held-out rater) — both small on commit, both large on SummEval. (iii) On commit What all arms sit at ρ ≈ .44 and the hard→chen gain is concentrated at the upper thresholds (t ≤ 2, 3): the gain is distributional sharpness/shape at the top of the scale, not location — consistent with the non-item-linked smoothing reading. Caveat: SummEval `perm` has fold 0 only (its lower RPS reflects the easier expert), so its row is not comparable to the two-fold arms.

**Pending**: SummEval fold 2 (chain6), then chain7 (seed replication, analysed with analysis_chain7.py), v3 heads reruns (certified), DeepSeek Why.

Same qualitative pattern (commit: soft adds .02 location over hard; SummEval: adds .09; perm_mode loses it), stated as an *exploratory explanation*, not a mechanism.

The D-ranking improvement under pmm/pmmx/shrunk_res (+0.014–0.017) is stated as "consistent with noisy residual-shape supervision in the four-rater setting" (pmm is a true bijection so target-entropy artefacts cannot explain it; pmmx and shrunk_res are not bijections). Title → *What Distributional Judge Training Uses from Human Rating Histograms: A Link-Randomization Audit*; estimand paragraph, abstract core and expanded do-not-write list placed at the top of PAPER_OUTLINE.md.

Provisional until v4.

## Hour 15c (18:10–18:50) — exact KL projection (KKT active-set enumeration) replaces the numerical optimizers

Codex round 7 vetoed "optimizer success required" (my trust-constr patch had dropped the check); restoring it made the self-test fail on feasible cases where SLSQP/trust-constr report non-convergence. Replaced both by the exact solution: by convexity the KL projection onto {Σq = 1, E[Y] = μ, q_z ≥ q_k} has the KKT form q_k = s·b_k e^{λk} for untied classes and q_j = c = s·G(λ) for the mode and the tied set, G = geometric mean of the tied classes' tilted values; λ solves the ratio equation (F₁ + G·J)/(F₀ + n_T·G) = μ by bisection (log-sum-exp stabilised), and the tie set is found by enumerating all 2^(K−1) candidates and keeping the KKT-consistent one (untied below the cap, tied multipliers ≥ 0). Self-test: K = 3/4/5 × 3,000 random histogram/prototype pairs — 0 violations, 0 failures; 3 of 9,000 cases fell back to the minimum-KL feasible candidate when no tie set was KKT-consistent to 1e-9 (counted as `projected_minkl`). Brute-force optimality spot check and 4,000 adversarial Dirichlet bases running. The one "failure" in my ad-hoc tests — (mode 2, mean 3.2) — is genuinely infeasible (max mean with mode 2 on 0..4 is 3.0), so raising is correct.
Consequence: every heads rerun that used the SLSQP/trust-constr versions (`_v3` commit Why / BW / DICES; `_v4` partial) is superseded; relaunched as `_v4` with the exact solver on commit Why/What, BW, DICES, SummEval coherence, Scalabrino, HelpSteer2 (certified mean2 / pmmx throughout). LoRA `mean2` arms so far used the unconstrained tilt (documented as a projection control, not rerun).
Solver validation complete: 4,000 adversarial Dirichlet bases → 0 failures (1,208 tilt, 2,792 projected); brute-force optimality spot check on 20 random feasible (base, mode, mean) cases with 30,000 random feasible points each → the KKT solution was never beaten (smallest gap best_random − KKT = +0.0156 nats). The constrained projection is now exact and certified.

## Hour 16 (18:50–19:50) — commit Why location effect replicates in raw RPS on splits 1 and 2 and in the DeepSeek family; exact-solver heads identical

**LoRA commit Why, three item splits (6 folds each) and the DeepSeek-coder-6.7B family (s0; perm_mode 2/6 folds so far), RAW | common | arm-specific**
| split / family | hard − chen | perm − chen | perm_mode − chen | perm_mode − hard |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen s0 | +0.0073 [+0.0047,+0.0099] / +0.0049 / +0.0008 | −0.0002 / −0.0002 / −0.0000 | +0.0005 [−0.0021,+0.0032] / +0.0033 / +0.0033 [+0.0013,+0.0054] | −0.0067 / −0.0016 / +0.0025 |
| Qwen s1 | +0.0093 [+0.0055,+0.0130] / +0.0083 / +0.0029 | −0.0002 / −0.0002 / −0.0002 | **+0.0061 [+0.0020,+0.0101] / +0.0069 / +0.0073 [+0.0031,+0.0114]** | −0.0031 / −0.0014 / +0.0044 |
| Qwen s2 | +0.0125 [+0.0079,+0.0174] / +0.0086 / +0.0043 | +0.0000 / −0.0001 / −0.0001 | **+0.0091 [+0.0057,+0.0128] / +0.0095 / +0.0088 [+0.0052,+0.0124]** | −0.0034 / +0.0008 / +0.0045 |
| DeepSeek s0 (perm_mode 2 folds) | +0.0091 [+0.0056,+0.0128] / +0.0023 / +0.0025 | −0.0005 / −0.0001 / −0.0001 | **+0.0060 [+0.0016,+0.0102] / +0.0055 / +0.0055 [+0.0023,+0.0085]** | −0.0030 / +0.0036 / +0.0038 |
Reading: the hour-13 worry ("the commit Why location effect exists only after calibration") was a split-0 feature. On splits 1 and 2 the mode-only permutation costs +0.006–0.009 RPS under all three estimands, and the second model family shows +0.006 (2 folds). The mode+mean permutation is within ±0.0005 of chen in every split, family and estimand. Caveat: the three splits share the item pool (overlapping test items) and each has one optimizer seed; the chain7 block adds orthogonal seeds (SummEval first, then commit).

**Heads, commit Why `_v4` (exact KKT projection, 0 violations; 30 cfgs)**: numerically identical to `_v3` (the v2 fallback was never triggered on this panel): pmmx RPS +0.0003±0.0005 / KL +0.005 / D-partial +0.017; pmm −0.0001 / +0.001 / +0.017; cond −0.0001 / −0.008 / +0.011; mean2 +0.0014 / +0.011 / +0.019; pmode +0.0092 / +0.063 / −0.026. Other panels' `_v4` reruns in progress.

**Good**: the location effect on commit Why is now a 2-of-3-splits + second-family result in the primary estimand; the mean-preserving null holds in 4/4 LoRA configurations. **Bad**: splits are not independent (shared items); seeds still single per split until chain7.

## Hour 16b (19:50–20:30) — Codex round 8 digest (reviews/codex_round8_2026-10-11.md) and actions

**Vetoes**: (1) "replicates in 2/3 splits + second family" → the honest language is *robustness across three overlapping Qwen item partitions (one optimizer seed, one realized permutation each) and a preliminary concordant second-family run (2/6 folds)*; split 0 is **partition sensitivity**, not an outlier; no formal heterogeneity test (overlapping items); do not say "destroyed the gain" (raw perm_mode still beats hard). (2) The exact projection was NOT exact: the 3 `projected_minkl` cases were genuine sub-optima at a KKT root at λ → +∞ (e.g. base (.060, .068, .424, .164, .285), mode 2, mean 3: returned (0,0,⅓,⅓,⅓) with KL .209, optimum (0,0,.405,.190,.405) with KL .152, tie set {2,4}); the random brute-force check cannot find boundary-face optima. → fixed: limiting distributions at λ = ±∞ constructed analytically with their KKT conditions, the min-KL fallback removed (hard-fail), Codex's case added as a regression test with an objective assertion. Label: "deterministic KKT active-set KL projection, constraint- and optimality-certified to numerical tolerance, relative to the 1e-9-floored base". (3) Table: show the perm − chen CIs (What s0 [−.00027, .00044]; Why s0–s2 [−.00072, .00031], [−.00080, .00030], [−.00044, .00048]; DeepSeek [−.00125, .00035]); mark DeepSeek "preliminary, 2/6 folds"; the common map must use a fixed roster {hard, chen, perm, perm_mode} across partitions → analysis_v3 patched and partitions recomputed. (4) Start chain7 now (SummEval block attacks the most damaging weakness); DeepSeek f2–5 resumed afterwards (chain8). The paired seeds are *joint seed realizations*, not orthogonal factors. (5) `analysis_block_summeval.py` frozen before any chain7 output (doc-clustered, fixed 3-arm common roster, refit inside bootstrap).
**Abstract core (Codex round 8)** adopted into PAPER_OUTLINE.md.
**Consequence for heads**: every `_v4` rerun that could have hit a boundary case is superseded → after the fixed solver passes its tests, relaunch as `_v5` with per-panel projection-status logging; rerun only panels whose targets differ (regeneration check) where feasible.
Solver (boundary-aware) self-test: Codex's regression case now returns the optimum (0, 0, .40477, .19047, .40477), KL .15212; K = 3/4/5 × 3,000 cases — 0 violations, 0 failures, no fallback path remains (the three former `projected_minkl` cases are now ordinary KKT solutions at the λ → +∞ boundary). Heads reruns relaunched as `_v5` on all seven ordinal panels with per-run projection-status and post-projection-dose logging; `_v4` outputs quarantined.

## Hour 17 (20:30–21:30) — dose audit: the heads' permutations matched on rater-indexed VECTORS, not histograms; effective dose was ~20 % on commit Why

The new projection-status log in `commit_why_v5` exposed it: `soft_pmmx` certificate — changed_fraction (matched items) 0.673, but **changed_after_projection 0.195**, and every "projection" returned the donor unchanged (`status_base` = all). Cause: `probe_mean2.permute` (and `probe_nominal`, `gxr_check`) built matching classes from the rater-indexed rating vector `tuple(Htr[i])`, so two items with different vectors but the *same histogram* (same multiset of 4 ratings) were "matched" and received an identical target. The LoRA trainer (`train_judge_v2`) matched on histogram tuples and was not affected. Consequence: in all heads runs so far (probe_mean, probe_mean2 v2–v5, probe_nominal) the reported pmm/pmmx/pmode intensities overstate the histogram-level dose; on commit Why the effective pmm dose was ≈ 20 % of training items (not 67 %). The heads' near-zero pmm contrasts were therefore measured at a low dose and are weaker evidence than stated; pmode (mode-only) is less affected because mode-only cells mix means, but still inflated. Fixed in all three scripts (histogram-class matching with a post-hoc assertion; TV dose logged). All heads reruns relaunched as `_v6` (ordinal panels) and `_v6` for the ChaosNLI m-sweep; the ChaosNLI case is less affected (100-position vectors vs m-annotation histograms — identical histograms are rare at m ≥ 10 but not at m = 5).
Lesson for the paper: report *post-intervention histogram change* (fraction and TV dose) for every permutation arm, never the matched fraction.

## Hour 18 (21:30–22:30) — dose audit extended to the SummEval LoRA trainer; G×R check done (6/8); commit Why v6 heads unchanged

**Bad.** The vector-matching bug was not confined to the heads: `train_judge_v3` (SummEval LoRA, the T3b headline block) also matched on rater-indexed vectors. Reproducing the s0 mappings: `perm` had 18–23 % of matched pairs with identical histograms, so its realised dose was 0.72–0.74 of training items (logged 0.91–0.94); `perm_mode` effective 0.96–0.98 (negligible). The commit LoRA trainer (`train_judge_v2`) matched on histogram classes with an assertion — unaffected. Action: fixed `train_judge_v3` (histogram classes, assertion, `n_tv_zero` certificate); stopped the chain7 driver before it reached the perm arms (the seed-2002 f2 `chen` run was allowed to finish — chen is unaffected); moved the s0 vector-matched `perm`/`perm_mode` to `s0_vecmatch`; launched `lora_chain7b.sh`: SummEval perm/perm_mode × {2001, 2002, 2000-rerun} × 3 folds (18 runs ≈ 3.5 h) → DeepSeek Why perm_mode f4, f5 (chain8 folded in) → commit s0 block. The frozen `analysis_block_summeval.py` runs unchanged once all three seeds are complete; T3b currently carries a DOSE NOTE and will be replaced by the block result.
**Intrinsic dose.** On commit Why (4 training ratings, 5-point scale) histogram-class matching changes only 22.6 % of training items under mode+mean preservation (v6 certificate) — mode and mean nearly determine a 4-rating histogram. This is a property of the panel, not a bug, and the paper must state it: the commit mode-and-mean-preserving null is a null on ≈ 23–34 % of items (LoRA 0.29–0.34 with 5 training ratings). SummEval (6–7 ratings) 0.81; dense panels ≈ 1.0.
**Good.** commit Why `_v6` (histogram-class) reproduces v5 within noise: pmm +0.0004±0.0005, pmmx −0.0002±0.0005, pmode +0.0083±0.0007, cond −0.0003, mean2 +0.0010, hard +0.0123. The bug did not change any heads conclusion on this panel; dose is now reported correctly (T2b). `commit_what_v6` running; summeval/bw/dices/scalabrino/hs2 + ChaosNLI m-sweep queued.
**G×R check (prespecified, T7).** Sign concordance 6/8, Spearman 0.62. All five panels with a clear location effect (SummEval ×2, DICES, commit Why, commit What) have G×R > 0.025; the three without (BW, HelpSteer2, Scalabrino) have |G×R| < 0.01, and the two sign mismatches are panels where both quantities are ≈ 0. Supports the location explanation as a necessary-condition account, not as an effect-size predictor (SummEval coherence has the largest G×R, commit Why the largest E). New discrepancy to state: heads show a location effect on commit What (+0.0056) that LoRA commit What did not.
**Next.** v6 heads for the remaining panels; SummEval block analysis when chain7b finishes the 18 runs; Codex round 9 (sent this hour) on the dose audit, the intrinsic-dose framing and the G×R wording.

SummEval coherence `_v6`: pmm −0.0008±0.0003, pmmx −0.0010±0.0003, pmode +0.0050±0.0006, sordm −0.0053±0.0010, mean2 −0.0021±0.0003 (dose pmm 0.728 / TV 0.38). ## Hour 20 (23:30–00:30) — commit heads-vs-LoRA bridge: discrepancy survives exact alignment; BW/DICES v6 in; chain7b perm arms done for seeds 2001/2002

**Bridge (new, `bridge_commit.py`, T8).** Heads trained on exactly the LoRA split-0 partition, LORO folds 0–5, with the LoRA's own perm_mode donor assignment (re-derived from train_judge_v2's cells and RNG; changed counts and TV match the LoRA certificates in 12/12 fold-criterion cells). Result: heads pmode − soft is positive in 12/12 folds (What +0.0038, fold SEM 0.0012; Why +0.0090, fold SEM 0.0019; seed SEM ≤ 0.0013), while the single-run LoRA perm_mode − chen is fold-heterogeneous with both signs (What −0.0007; Why +0.0005; per-fold range −0.0086 … +0.0059). So the heads/LoRA disagreement on commit What is NOT a split or permutation-draw artefact; it is pipeline-level. Two constraints: LoRA is 0.4–0.6× as sensitive to target information (hard − chen vs heads hard − soft), and LoRA fold-to-fold spread (SD ≈ 0.005) equals the heads' mean effect, so single-realisation optimizer variance is a live explanation. The chain7b commit block (seeds 2001/2002 × folds 0–2) will measure it. Consequence for the Why story too: the "location effect on Why" in the LoRA is +0.0005 on the original partition (folds mixed) — the partition-1/2 and DeepSeek results carry it; the heads see it on every fold. Paper wording updated in PAPER_OUTLINE.
**v6 heads.** BW `_v6` is numerically identical to `_v3` (97 raters: every vector and histogram unique, so vector vs histogram classes coincide — the bug had zero effect on dense panels). DICES `_v6`: pmmx −0.0018±0.0007, pmm −0.0005, cond −0.0016, mean2 −0.0009, sordm +0.0429 (replicates the SORD failure), but pmode flipped sign: −0.0015±0.0008 (v3) → +0.0022±0.0011 (v6); the G×R run gave +0.0039±0.0014. These are different permutation/optimizer streams of the same code → the DICES mode-only effect is within the draw-to-draw variability and must not be called a location effect either way; descriptive SEM over 30 dependent configs understates it (Codex round 9 veto confirmed empirically). Scalabrino running; hs2, ChaosNLI m-sweep, T4 reruns queued.
**chain7b.** SummEval perm (histogram matching) done for seeds 2001/2002, 0 tracebacks; perm_mode seed-2001 running; 8 runs left (s0 perm/perm_mode rerun + perm_mode 2001/2002 remaining) ≈ 2.5 h → block analysis around 03:00. DeepSeek still 4/6 (queued after the block).
**Assessment.** Good: the bridge converts a vague "pipelines disagree" into a sharp statement (alignment does not help; sensitivity and optimizer variance remain), and it was cheap (CPU, 4 min). Bad: it weakens the LoRA commit evidence further — single-seed LoRA fold contrasts are noisier than our CIs (conditional on the trained model) imply; the paper must say the LoRA intervals condition on one optimizer realisation. Change: treat the chain7b commit optimizer block as critical path alongside the SummEval block; consider heads as the primary multi-panel instrument and LoRA as the confirmation on two panels, rather than the reverse. Codex round 10 sent on these three points.

Both consistent with the earlier runs. All 7 ordinal `_v6` panels are now in T2b; ChaosNLI `_v6` m-sweep running (m = 5 first); T4 positive-control reruns queued after it.

## Hour 23 (02:30–03:50) — corrected SummEval block in: mode+mean-preserving replacement equivalent within ±.001; mode-only costs +0.012 in every seed and fold

**SummEval block (frozen analysis_block_summeval.py, B = 10,000; results/lora/summeval_coherence8/analysis_block_summeval.{log,json}).** 3 joint seed realisations × 3 expert folds × {chen, perm, perm_mode}, all histogram-class matched (perm dose 0.81–0.83, TV 0.34–0.35, n_tv_zero = 0 in every cell).
- perm − chen: RAW −0.0001 [−0.0011, +0.0011]; common +0.0000 [−0.0006, +0.0008]; arm +0.0003 [−0.0015, +0.0022]; seeds −0.0007 / +0.0007 / −0.0002; folds −0.0011 / 0.0000 / +0.0009. Equivalence by the 90 % criterion inside ±.001 / .002 / .003 (bands post hoc; ±.002 frozen after seed-2000 folds).
- perm_mode − chen: RAW +0.0120 [+0.0086, +0.0155]; common +0.0077 [+0.0053, +0.0109]; arm +0.0084 [+0.0051, +0.0121]; seeds +0.0095 / +0.0130 / +0.0136; folds +0.0172 / +0.0116 / +0.0073. common − raw −0.0045 [−0.0074, −0.0015]: calibration attenuates but does not remove the location penalty.
- Calibration grid: λ = 0.5 (max) hit in 53 % of refits → extended-grid sensitivity (λ ≤ 0.9, B = 4,000) launched as analysis_block_summeval_lamext.py (non-frozen sensitivity; raw estimand unaffected).
- Matching nuance discovered from the certificates: changed fraction and mean TV are identical across permutation seeds (class-to-class pairing deterministic given class sizes; seeds randomise within-class donor identity; hashes differ). Disclosed in draft §2.2; wording asked of Codex.
T3b replaced; vector-matched single-seed row demoted to an appendix audit line; draft §4 SummEval frozen.

## Hour 39 (17:20–18:20) — αNLI is the zero-dose limit and behaves accordingly; nominal study = MNLI + SNLI (+ αNLI as a pipeline check)

**ChaosNLI αNLI (`_v7`, K = 2).** The final-dose certificate shows what K = 2 implies: retaining the modal class and its mass fixes the entire distribution, so every pzc target equals the original histogram (final TV-all 0.000 at m = 5/10/20/50 although 0.00/0.06/0.47/0.83 of items were nominally mapped). The observed pzc − soft KL contrasts are −0.001/−0.004/+0.002/+0.000 (±0.002–0.006) — exactly the null a zero-dose intervention must give; this is a built-in negative control for the pipeline, reported as such and not counted as nominal evidence. zc − soft is also ≈ 0 (two statistics determine two classes); pmode − soft +0.005/+0.008/+0.004/+0.002; hard − soft +0.022…+0.030. The aggregator now tolerates undefined residual-share ranks (K = 2).
**Tables/figures/draft.** T5 has three blocks; Fig. 4 three subplots; §3 nominal paragraph gains the αNLI sentence; captions and outline updated. check_consistency 0 unmatched; manifest refreshed.
**Pipeline.** popquorn_pol5 features 700/3690 (CPU, ~4 h more), then csc6 and emobank; heads chain waiting. GPU 0 still faulted; GPU 1 unused (foreign job).
**Assessment.** Good: the degenerate case confirms that the dose certificate predicts the contrast (zero dose → zero effect), which is a nice internal validity check to put in the paper. Bad: nothing on the LoRA side can move without a GPU. Change: none.

### 2026-11-01 19:40 — Codex brainstorm (gpt-6.1-sol max): new methods and angles; two closely related papers verified
Verified on arXiv: WIMHF (Movva, Milli, Min, Pierson, 2510.26202) learns annotator-specific weights (Gaussian prior at the global coefficient) over one SAE-derived feature (paragraphs vs lists) on Community Alignment for *seen* high-volume annotators, AUC +1.1 % at k = 16, no permutation control, active sampling helps at small k — a close precedent for SAP that must be cited and reproduced under our protocol. Chen, Zheng & Zhu (2610.30763) already compare stated vs revealed preferences on PRISM (stated priorities do not predict feature weights; personal info +0.13 points) — the author's idea A is largely taken. Codex ranking: (1) transferable heterogeneity — WIMHF comparison, support/query cross-prompt covariance diagnostic (same vs different prompt context, with population-misspecification, inference and power controls), transfer-trained adaptation; (2) explanations as observations of the preference dimension (CA free-text reasons); (3) assessment vs trade-off-weight decomposition (MultiPref); companion: conditional likelihood given |Y| that cancels symmetric scale offsets exactly. Decision: start with (1) under a new frozen protocol (Stage 11). References added to the bib.

- 用户同意做下一步问题 1、2。PROTOCOL_STAGE14.md：S14-1 Jester 真实数据 positive control（三级：L0 线性 CF 参照 / L1 管线 DL + item-identity 特征 + 已见笑话 / L2 管线 DL + 每折选 PCA 维度 + 新笑话；k=20 一致率 own−μ 下界 > +1 点且 DL 增益超过 DF；预设读法 R1–R4）；S14-2 按总体模型 |logit| 的 20% 分位把测试对分成 contested / rest，看 TRV 增益是否集中（LL 共同主指标）。
